### Source: https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/gc-rise-for-freedom-in-global-campaign-against-gender-based-violence

# GC Rise for Freedom in Global Campaign Against Gender-Based Violence
[Image: GC Rise for Freedom in Global Campaign Against Gender-Based Violence]
[Alumni](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/alumni/) [Campus](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/campus/) [GAD](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/gad/) [IPDEA](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ipdea/) [NSTP](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/nstp/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Thursday, February 13, 2025**
Gordon College joined the global movement “One Billion Rising: Rise for Freedom” on February 14, 2025 at the Olongapo City Tapinac Oval Sports Complex as the students, faculty, and alumni united in dance and solidarity to raise awareness about violence against women and girls.
One Billion Rising is the most extensive mass action to end violence against women in human history which began in 2012 worldwide.
This advocacy campaign was further adapted by the Philippines on February 10, 2025.
Additionally, the campaign was based on the statistics from the United Nations that one in three women globally will experience violence in their lifetime, and with a world population of 7 billion, this translates to over one billion women and girls.
The event, organized by the Institutional Planning, Development, and External Affairs (IPDEA) through the Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion Unit, in partnership with the National Service Training Program (NSTP) and the Gordon College Alumni Association, Inc., saw enthusiastic participation from the entire college community.
Dr. Imelda OP. Soriano, OIC-College President, emphasized the institution’s commitment to ending all forms of violence against women and girls. “As a woman, we need to rise and rise for what is right for us,” the newly-pledged college president stated.
“In this activity, we will dance, we will rise because we want to demand justice. Together, we will break this silence through dancing because this is not just an act of resistance, but it is our expression of our unyielding spirit, our joy, and our collective power to fight injustices against women.”
Furthermore, the event featured a mass dance to the One Billion Rising anthem, “Break the Chain.”
Harvey Bolfango, a first-year BSED English student, highlighted the importance of student involvement. “It is important to every student of Gordon College to be part of this event because it embodies a lot of respect, dignity, transparency, and more awareness of certain causes like violence against women and other girls.”
Additionally, John Andrei Facultad, a first-year BSED English student, emphasized the importance of speaking up against violence. “Don’t be afraid to speak up if you have ever been attacked. Kahit pa sabihin natin na verbal ‘yan or hindi gano’n kalala. But violence is still violence, and it should be addressed.”
Sir Joseph Atencio, OIC Head of the DEI Unit, explained the partnership between NSTP, the GC Alumni, and the DEI Unit.
“Gusto natin ipakita na si Gordon College is for the protection of women’s rights, and especially ‘yung mga children, mga batang babae. Kasi ang usaping women empowerment (and) women’s rights is not only for women alone. Bagkus buong society, buong sector is tumutulong, and nag-participate sa pagtaguyod and pagpapaunladng karapatan ng kababaihan,” he stated.
He also affirmed Gordon College’s ongoing commitment to diversity through the DEI unit and its pursuit of inclusive education.
As the event concluded, Dr. Darwin Paguio, Vice President of Academic Affairs, echoed the call to action, stating, “Rising is not an option. It is our duty. Let us rise for ourselves, for each other, and for generations to come. Rise up, stand firm, and let our collective courage illuminate the path for a brighter tomorrow. The time is now. Let us rise, Gordon College.”

# CEAS Mentors, ibinida ang ginto sa Standard Category
[Image: CEAS Mentors, ibinida ang ginto sa Standard Category]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Monday, September 15, 2025**
GORDON COLLEGE, OLONGAPO CITY – Umangat ang kahusayan ng College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS) Mentors sa taunang Dance Sports Competition Standard Category, matapos matagumpay na hinataw ang dalawang katunggali sa entablado sa pamamagitan ng tatlong klasikong sayaw.
Sinubukan ang kanilang husay at indak sa Waltz na nagpakita ng pino at walang-kupas na galaw, sa Tango na umigting sa matinding ekspresyon, at Quickstep na sumubok sa bilis, koordinasyon, at tibay ng bawat pares.
Sa kabila ng dikit na laban, naging kapansin-pansin ang stage presence, chemistry, kontrolado ngunit makapangyarihang galaw ni Jieann Cahanding at Ivan Russel Bahala mula sa CEAS Mentors na nagpakita ng malinis na footwork hanggang sa makabagbag na emosyon sa entablado. Sinundan ito ng CHTM (College of Hospitality and Tourism Management) Sharks sa ikalawang pwesto at CAHS (College of Allied and Health Studies) Tigers sa ikatlong pwesto.
“Aside from being student athletes, of course. Kami kasi ng partner ko first time partner, yung adjustment doon na tinuturuan ko siya kasi hindi rin siya talaga dance sport athlete, pero yung nakakamangha sa kaniya ay madali siya turuan, saad ni Jieann ukol sa hinarap nilang problema bago tumungtong sa entablado.
“Yung struggle namin ay ‘yong time management medyo mahirap kasi tuwing hapon lang kami nakakapag practice,” dagdag pa niya.
“Sobrang saya po na nakakaiyak. As a first-timer malaking challenge sa akin yun at malaking achievement,” ayon kay Bahala patungkol sa naramdaman matapos sungkitin ang kampeyonato.
Sa pagtatapos ng patimpalak, hindi lamang talento sa pagsasayaw ang naipakita ng mga kalahok. Bawat kolehiyo ay nagbigay ng kani-kaniyang tatak—mula sa makukulay na costume, malinis na footwork, hanggang sa mga koreograpiyang may hatid na emosyon at kwentong Gcians. | via Lianne Malazarte/The FOREFRONT

# Gordon College Receives 21 Certificates of Accreditation at NNQAA 2026 Summit
[Image: Gordon College Receives 21 Certificates of Accreditation at NNQAA 2026 Summit]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Friday, March 6, 2026**
Gordon College proudly received Certificates of Accreditation for its 21 ALCUCOA-accredited programs during the NNQAA 2nd Quality Assurance Summit and Conferment Ceremony for Accredited Programs and Institutions 2026, with the theme “Responsible Future: Quality Assurance in Action.”
The recognition was conferred by the National Network of Quality Assurance Agencies (NNQAA), Inc., led by Chairman Dr. Raymundo P. Arcega, during the summit held March 6, 2026 at The Heritage Hotel Manila.
The award was received by Dr. Imelda D.P. Soriano, College President; Dr. Kristin Joy A. Mendoza, Vice President for Institutional Planning, Development and External Affairs; and Mr. Joseph Angelo B. Pusing, Officer-in-Charge of the Center for Quality Assurance and Accreditation, representing Gordon College.
These recognitions highlight the College’s continued commitment to quality education, academic excellence, and institutional development.
Level III Accredited Programs
1. Bachelor of Science in Business Administration – Financial Management
2. Bachelor of Science in Business Administration – Human Resource Management
3. Bachelor of Science in Business Administration – Marketing Management
4. Bachelor of Science in Customs Administration
5. Bachelor of Secondary Education – Mathematics
6. Bachelor of Secondary Education – Social Studies
7. Bachelor of Science in Nursing
Level II Accredited Programs
1. Bachelor of Elementary Education
2. Bachelor of Science in Accountancy
3. Bachelor of Science in Computer Science
4. Bachelor of Secondary Education – English
5. Bachelor of Secondary Education – Science
6. Bachelor of Science in Information Technology
Level I Accredited Programs
1. Bachelor of Arts in Communication
2. Bachelor of Early Childhood Education
3. Bachelor of Secondary Education – Filipino
4. Bachelor of Science in Hospitality Management
5. Bachelor of Science in Midwifery
6. Bachelor of Science in Tourism Management
Candidate Status Programs
1. Bachelor of Culture and Arts Education
2. Bachelor of Physical Education
The College extends its deepest gratitude to Hon. Rolen C. Paulino Jr., City Mayor; Hon. Cristabelle Marie C. Paulino, Chairperson of the Committee on Education; Dr. Imelda D.P. Soriano, College President; the City Officials, the Board of Trustees, Vice Presidents, College Officials, Deans, Assistant Deans, Program Chairs, Management Council Members, Faculty Members, Administrative Personnel, and all our stakeholders for their continued support in strengthening Gordon College’s pursuit of quality assurance and academic excellence.

# Initial Presentation of the Project METRO
[Image: Initial Presentation of the Project METRO]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Thursday, January 29, 2026**
Gordon College – College of Computer Studies (GC-CCS), in partnership with the Department of Trade and Industry (DTI) – Zambales, successfully conducted the initial product presentation of LPCC Ikot Palengke and Fair Trade Law Monitoring activities under PROJECT METRO: Monitoring Enforcement & Tracking Resource Online on January 29, 2026.
The presentation was piloted by OneDev, a team of third-year BSIT student developers—Marina Camaso, Paulo Cordova, Nikolai Nepomuceno, Earl Romeo Ordovez, Aaron Paul Sario, and Seann Patrick Tamondong—who showcased a prototype of a digital system designed to enhance government operations across province-wide public markets and retail establishments. The system aims to strengthen consumer protection, enforce fair trade laws, and improve efficient public service delivery through accurate data collection and monitoring.
The activity was attended by Gordon College administrators, headed by Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano, College President; Dr. Darwin P. Paguio, Vice President for Academic Affairs; Dr. Erlinda S. Casela-Abarintos, Vice President for Administration and Finance; Dr. Kristin Joy A. Mendoza, Vice President for Institutional Planning and External Affairs; Dr. Ediric D. Gadia, Vice President for Research Development and Community Extension Services; Dr. Precious De Castro, Coordinator of the International Affairs Unit; Prof. Ronnie D. Luy, Dean of the College of Computer Studies; Prof. Armilyn T. Martinez, Assistant Dean of the College of Computer Studies; and Prof. Reynaldo G. Bautista Jr., Program Coordinator of the Bachelor of Science in Information Technology. Also present were officials from DTI Zambales, led by Mr. Neil John Fabay, Division Chief of the Consumer Protection Division, along with Ms. Marilou Benonto and Ms. Melody Tangonan, Senior Trade Industry Development Specialists.
This initiative highlights Gordon College’s commitment to innovation and technology-driven governance, emphasizing its role in advancing the institution’s global technology and general assistance initiatives.
Gordon College extends its appreciation to all participating officials, partners, and stakeholders for their continued support in advancing development, research, and public service through technology.

# Gordon College Faculty Join Basic FSL Training to Promote Inclusive Education
[Image: Gordon College Faculty Join Basic FSL Training to Promote Inclusive Education]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Friday, August 29, 2025**
Faculty members from the College of Education, Arts and Sciences (CEAS) and the College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) with Mr. Joseph Atencio-OIC, Head of Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion (DEI) Unit proudly took part in a three-day Basic Filipino Sign Language (FSL) Training held last August 26–28, 2025 at the SMX Convention Center, Olongapo City. The event was organized by the Olongapo City Persons with Disability Affairs Office (PDAO) in collaboration with the Department of Education – Olongapo City Division Office.
Throughout the training, participants engaged in interactive and hands-on activities designed to equip educators with the skills to communicate effectively with the Deaf—nurturing a more inclusive, and compassionate learning environment.
This initiative not only strengthened our instructors’ professional competencies but also reaffirmed their dedication to accessibility, diversity, and inclusivity in education—a true reflection of Gordon College’s commitment to ensuring that no learner is left behind.

# LIMC History
Gordon College Library started in 1999 when the former Olongapo City Colleges, now Gordon College, put up a curricular program for Computer Studies and Business Management and Accountancy. With only a few books and mostly computer subjects in hand, Mrs. Remedios Sapuan, who was then a Utility worker, took charge of the library operation with the assistance of the Administrative Staff.
The Library moved four times to different locations in the building and its fourth was in the former KHG Hall. When the library was first opened, books were in the cabinet with glass doors and were locked. The library was using the close shelf system, where stacks cannot be directly accessed by users. The students should approach the assigned staff when borrowing books and it was then the bookshelf was opened to get the book needed.
In 2002, Thelma Ramos, a clerk aid of the Assessor’s Office, was transferred to the College Library to assist Mrs. Sapuan in the library operation. According to Mrs. Sapuan, a certain instructor, in the person of Mr. Sydney Maglalang, was temporarily assigned to the library. Services of the staff in the City Library were borrowed in the processing of books acquired for the College Library. In June 2003, the first College Librarian was hired, Ms. Maria Corazon D. Maneja as a casual position. Later she was promoted to a permanent position. The problem was by her was the book organization, the right classification of books, classification tools (DDC) Dewey Decimal Classification System and there were no catalog cards and cabinets. Some of the books were infested by termites which were already problems before but were not treated completely. Books were thenr eorganized, classified, and cataloged and were assigned new numbers using DD Classification tools that were borrowed from the Ci4ty Library. The circulation counter and grills dividing the books stacks and reading area were removed. Shelves were distributed and fastened on all library walls to make the books accessible to the users and to widen the reading area. Heavily termite-infested books were removed and deleted from the records. New books were formed. Newspapers were compiled with the date labeled and put on display. In 2006, books from Australia were received by the library through the Rotary Club of Olongapo. Books were found to be outdated and mostly for High School and Elementary level students. In the same year, another book donation was received from Asia Foundations, Inc. Books became very useful to patrons because of their general content. In March 2007, Ms. Betty Fielder, a friend of Mrs. Dely Aristorenas, former College Administrator, donated books and magazines for use by HRM students and faculty. Two air conditioners were installed to replace the former electric fans for ventilation and besides its small space, library patrons flocked the library especially nursing students who occupied the most number of enrollees. The library had then 8,361 volumes of books as of October 2007.
In the summer of 2010, the librarian during one of the EXECOM (ExecutiveCommittee) meetings, suggested removing over duplicated copies of books to provide spaces for new arrivals. Enough copies from weeded books were retained. 2,958 books were discarded and returned to GSO (Government Supply Office) but the latter suggested just donating them to the City Library.
In 2012, Gordon College&#39;s 13th Founding Anniversary, a Give-a-Book Project was launched and spearheaded by the College Library to collect tertiary level books from students, faculty, alumni, and friends of Gordon College. As of this date, May 6, 2013, we have collected 182 book donations.
On August 7, 2012, when the first knee-deep flood entered the College Library, books located on the first two-row level shelves were partially damaged but were restored. But on September 23, 2013, during a chest-deep heavy flood of Olongapo City, almost 2/3 of the library materials were soaked in the mud and water and devastated and discarded. Furniture which was made of light materials, files, and newly acquired books was included, but other computers were restored. Due to the kindness of City Mayor Rolen C. Paulino, he approved a 1 million budget for the purchase of new general education books, professional books, and journals. The first set of books was delivered in December 2013. Another era was opened for Gordon College Library for its transfer to the new building funded and built by SM in 2015-2016. The official transfer began on June 24, 2016, until July of the same year. The new and much wider library which is located on the fourth floor was opened to students on August 1, 2016, the start of the first semester, the Academic Year 2016- 2017.
Mr. Marlon A. Atencio recommended to the Executive Council the change of the library name into “Library and Instructional Media Center” for the reason that the library is now having a media center that supports the educational needs of the library and it was approved by the Executive Council spearheaded by the College President, Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano last May 31, 2018.

# GC Color Run Kicks Off 27th Founding Anniversary
[Image: GC Color Run Kicks Off 27th Founding Anniversary]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Sunday, February 22, 2026**
The annual GC FUNdation RUN marked the start of Gordon College’s 27th Founding Anniversary, held on February 22, 2026, at the East Tapinac Oval Track.
The event gathered members of the Gordon College Faculty Association (GCFAS), administrative staff, students, alumni, and community participants under the pre-foundation day celebration motif, “Color Run.”
The program commenced with the assembly of participants, followed by an opening ceremony that featured welcome messages from Dr. Katherine Yap and Dr. Imelda D.P. Soriano. A Zumba warm-up session, led by administrators, faculty members, and students from the Bachelor of Physical Education (BPED), energized attendees ahead of the race.
The run officially started at 6:30 AM as participants filled the track dressed in white shirts and athletic attire, ready to be splashed with vibrant colored powder and water.
More than a display of color and movement, this year’s FUNdation RUN is a colorful display of the institution’s commitment to promoting wellness, unity, and community relations while marking the upcoming foundation celebration with lively and memorable moments. | via Sam Andrea R. # GC honors DASArving alumni, highlights iconic “first-times” in 4th DASA
[Image: GC honors DASArving alumni, highlights iconic “first-times” in 4th DASA]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, October 14, 2025**
SMX OLONGAPO CENTRAL— The 4th Distinguished Alumni Service Awards (DASA) 2025 gathered alumni who paved innovation and inspiration for Gordon College on October 11, held at SMX Olongapo Central.
Mr. Joseph Pusing, former Gordon College Alumni Association Inc. (GCAAI) President, proudly presented all the first-time projects that happened on the initiative of giving back to their alma mater: Alumni cards, Financial support for the student council and organizations, Cosme Y. Sering Library, and a fifty thousand cash donation for the College of Business and Accountancy (CBA) Department for the extension of rooms for accreditation.
“One of the highlights of our term is the donation of the alumni association’s newest student lounge, which was blessed on August 15, 2025. This will not be possible without the help and guidance of everyone,” he stated.
Also, Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano, the college president, exclaimed in her keynote speech that the world needs someone like them who wagers unselfish services in a world full of selfishness.
Furthermore, Ms. Rose Anne Cañeda Dumalo, alumna from the Bachelor of Science in Education – English (BSEd-English) Batch 2015, expressed in her speech that titles and recognition will not define us but the people that we serve along the way.
“Higit sa pagiging magaling ay ang pagiging mabuti. You may be the best in the field, but if you lack character, people will remember how you treated them more than how well you did your job,” she added.
Moreover, Ms. Ellinor T. Manaois-Yco, Bachelor of Science in Nursing (BSN) Batch 2011; Mr. Arben R. Cauilan, Bachelor of Science in Customs Administration (BSCA) Batch 2011; Nikki Antonette Chieng Carlos, Bachelor of Science in Tourism (BSTM); and Mr. Willy R. Antigo, Master of Arts in Education (MAEd) Batch 2019, were awarded with recognitions and plaques for their outstanding service and contributions.
In addition, Ms. Lovelyn P. Ceralde, DASA 2025 Overall Chairperson, extended her warm welcome and gratitude to the alumni, stakeholders, and those who have been bringing their best to provide a more quality education and experience inside the institution.
Finally, the program ended with an oath-taking for the newly elected GCAAI officers and board of trustees for the fiscal year 2025 – 2026. | via John Patrick Mateo/The FOREFRONT

# Responsibilities of the Data Subjects
Associated to the exercise of rights are responsibilities to respect the privacy of others and comply with Gordon College’s regulations in its administration of data privacy.
Data subjects shall have the following responsibilities:
1. Respect the data privacy rights of others;
2. Report any suspected security incident or personal data breach to Gordon College Data Protection Officer by sending an email or accomplishing OCP-DPU Form No. 4;
3. Provide Gordon College with true and accurate personal data and other information. Before submitting personal data of different people to Gordon College, obtain the consent of such people.
4. Not disclose to any unauthorized party any non-public confidential, sensitive or personal information obtained or learned in confidence from Gordon College; and
5. Abide by the policies, guidelines and rules of Gordon College on data privacy, information security, records management, research and ethical conduct. From time to time, check for and comply with updates on these policies, guidelines and rules. Students may also refer to their Student Handbook.

# 3rd Distinguished Alumni Service Award: Call for Nomination 2024
[Image: 3rd Distinguished Alumni Service Award: Call for Nomination 2024]
[Alumni](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/alumni/)
**Wednesday, January 24, 2024**
## THE 3RD GORDON COLLEGE DISTINGUISHED ALUMNI SERVICE AWARD (GC-DASA) FOR THE YEAR 2024
**GENERAL INFORMATION AND GUIDELINES**
Pursuant to Gordon College Alumni Association, Inc. (GCAAI) Board Resolution No. 4, series of 2022, the GCAAI establishes the **DISTINGUISHED ALUMNI SERVICE AWARD (DASA)** to honor Gordon College graduates who have excelled both in their professional and personal careers and who have served as an example to other alumni, students, and the community, both locally and globally. Starting this year, the GCAAI Board of Trustees will recognize the exemplary service and distinguished achievements that brought honor to the Institution and have made significant contributions to society. The GC-DASA recipients will exemplify the core values that the Institution uphold since its establishment: **Exceptional Character, Remarkable Excellence, And Unselfish Service.**
The GC-DASA shall be the highest honor bestowed by the Alumni Association and will be an event to be celebrated annually as part of the GC Founding Anniversary and/or GC Alumni Homecoming.
## GENERAL GUIDELINES:
1. Only graduates of Gordon College (or GC, formerly Olongapo City Colleges) are eligible for the GC-DASA. The nominee shall be a member of the Gordon College Alumni Association, Inc. as confirmed by its Board.
2. Any GC graduate, faculty member, or stakeholders may make nominations for the GC-DASA. Self–nominations are accepted. The nominee shall accomplish the Official Nomination Form to be provided by the GCAAI. No limitation will be placed on the total number of nominations accepted for consideration.
3. The nominee shall have no pending administrative or criminal case upon nomination. He/she shall exhibit a high degree of character & integrity.
4. Only one recipient per college/program will be awarded. In case the college/program will have no nominees/recipients, the awarding ceremony will still commence. Likewise, the GCAAI Board of Trustees can directly, without prejudice to the respective college, nominate and evaluate for such an award though the college/program have already a candidate.
5. Only living candidates are considered. In the event of the death of a candidate between notification of the award and the award ceremony, the award may be given posthumously with the permission of the family and the GCAAI. In the event that the awardee declines to accept the award and/or fails to attend the awarding ceremonies, the GCAAI reserves the right to withdraw the award.
6. A GC graduate sitting as a member of the selection committee or a member of the GCAAI Board of Trustees is not eligible to receive the award. This individual is eligible for nomination after a one-year waiting period from the date he/she step down from his/her role.
7. Recipients are only eligible for one GC-DASA in a lifetime.
8. The recipients will receive a plaque or certificate of recognition and will be posted on the official FB page of the GCAAI. It will announce the annual recipients through various methods and honor each recipient with the appropriate recognition.
9. If for any reason, the GCAAI is not able to conduct the Awards as planned (including due to tampering, unauthorized intervention, fraud, technical difficulties, pandemic, or any other causes beyond the control of the organizer which impact the administration, security, fairness, integrity or proper conduct of the Awards), then they reserve the right in their sole discretion to make any reasonable variation to the Awarding Ceremony.
10. The Nomination Committee and/or GCAAI Board of Trustees may disqualify any nominee that they reasonably determine has breached any of the terms and conditions or otherwise engaged in unlawful or other improper conduct that may bring the Awards into disrepute or jeopardize the fair and proper conduct of the Awards.
11. Other guidelines may be promulgated as decided by the GCAAI Board of Trustees.
## NOMINATION COMMITTEE:
The Nomination Committee (NC) may be organized by the Dean/OIC in every college and/or the GCAAI. The committee must be composed of at least three (3) members from their respective colleges. The total count of committee members should be an odd number. The chairperson of the NC shall be the Dean or its duly authorized representative and for the GCAAI, any member can initiate such nomination. No member of the NC must be related within the second degree of consanguinity or affinity to any of the nominees/candidates for awards. If the Dean/OIC is related within the second degree of consanguinity or affinity to any of the candidates for awards, the Dean/OIC must inhibit him/herself from participating in the process. The approval shall come from the person next in rank.
The NC shall:
1. Abide by the criteria set forth by the GCAAI.
2. For nominees endorsed by the College, it shall issue a certificate of authenticity to all the documents that will be submitted to the GCAAI.
3. Deliberate on the qualifications of the nominees for the awards based on the criteria and the documents submitted.
4. Recommend to the GCAAI Board of Trustees or its Alumni Association President the result of nomination for approval.
5. Recommend to the GCAAI Board of Trustees or its Alumni Association President the resolution on any related issue that may arise from the results of the awards.
6. Ensure that the guidelines stipulated herein are followed.
## NOMINATION AND SELECTION PROCESS:
1. Complete the online/hard copy Nomination Form. This also forms part of the terms and conditions.
2. Nominators will receive an auto-generated copy via email of what they submitted, i.e., the nomination form including any attachments. In any case, it is not available, the NC may determine what means of notification it will conduct to the nominators to confirm the submission of the nomination form and its attachments.
3. Nominees and nominators will be contacted by the NC/GCAAI within two days of receipt of the nomination to acknowledge receipt.
4. A nominee may be asked to clarify or submit further information and evidence to assist the NC/GCAAI in assessing the nomination at any stage of the selection process.
5. The NC/GCAAI may also seek verification of the nominee’s achievements from their current or previous employer or other relevant individual or organization representative. By being nominated for this Award, the nominees are giving consent to the NC/GCAAI to contact these individuals to verify nominations.
6. Nominations will be reviewed and shortlisted by the NC/GCAAI and nominees who have not been shortlisted will be notified. The GC-DASA candidates will be asked to submit a brief profile for this purpose. The candidate shall garner a total score of 60 points to be included in the shortlist. If a nominee cannot meet the required points, he/she is automatically excluded from the shortlist.
7. Every member of the NC will recommend/endorse the final candidate to the GCAAI President and then, the GCAAI Board of Trustees, sitting as Awards Selection Committee, will issue a confirmation through a Board Resolution. The report on the results of the NC shall be signed by all members of the committee and certified by the chairperson.
8. In any case that such protest will be filed during the confirmation of the final candidates, the final decision on the protest shall be decided by the GCAAI Board of Trustees. The GCAAI Board of Trustees’ decision is final on all matters relating to the nomination process and the selection of award recipients, and they are not required to provide feedback as to why a nomination was unsuccessful.
**CRITERIA:**

# Clean As You Go & Bring Your Own Trash Policy
[Image: Clean As You Go & Bring Your Own Trash Policy]
**Friday, August 8, 2025**
Instructions for Gordon College Students: Clean As You Go & Bring Your Own Trash Policy
Purpose:
To foster a clean and respectful campus environment while promoting sustainability through personal responsibility for waste.
Before Class or Activity:
1. Prepare Your Materials:
– Bring Your Own Trash: Carry a small, reusable bag or container to collect any waste you generate during class or activities. This could be a sturdy tote, a lunch bag, or any suitable container.
– Plan for Snacks/Drinks: If you plan to consume food or beverages, pack them in a way that minimizes waste (e.g., reusable containers, bottles).
During Class or Activity:
2. Maintain Your Space:
– Keep Your Area Clean: Throughout the session, stay vigilant about your surroundings. Avoid leaving papers, wrappers, or any personal items strewn about.
– Immediate Clean-Up: If you create a mess (accidental spills, paper cuts, etc.), address it promptly. If cleaning supplies are available, use them to tidy up immediately.
3. Responsible Waste Management:
– Use Your Reusable Container: Whenever you have waste, place it in your reusable bag or container. Keep it nearby for easy access.
– Respect Shared Spaces: Be mindful of not just your space but also those around you. Dispose of any common area waste (e.g., napkins from community snacks) in your own trash.
After Class or Activity:
4. Final Cleanup:
– Inspect Your Area:
Before leaving, take a moment to ensure your seating area and any communal spaces (tables, chairs) are tidy.
– Empty Your Trash into Appropriate Bins: When you leave the classroom or activity, empty your reusable container at designated trash or recycling bins outside the classroom (if available). Take anything that belongs to you with you.
5. Dispose Responsibly:
– Recycle When Possible: If your waste is recyclable, place it in the recycling bin. Be sure to check the college’s recycling guidelines to ensure proper disposal.
Additional Responsibilities:
6. Encourage a Culture of Cleanliness:
– Remind Peers:If you notice a classmate leaving a mess, gently remind them or offer to help.
– Lead by Example: Demonstrating your commitment to the “Clean As You Go” policy can inspire your peers to do the same.
7. Feedback and Improvement:
– Participate in discussions or feedback sessions organized by the college to share your insights on how the Clean As You Go policy is working.
8. Engage with Initiatives:
– Stay updated on any college-led environmental initiatives or clean-up days, and participate actively when you can.
By adhering to these instructions, you contribute to creating a cleaner, more sustainable campus environment while developing valuable habits of personal responsibility and community stewardship. Thank you for doing your part!

# LIMC Policies
## **GENERAL POLICIES**
* All bona fide members of the institution can access the library for the whole term after completing the library registration process.
* A library borrower’s card is issued by the library staff upon borrowing books.  
  Note: requirements for issuance of library borrower’s card are: recent 1×1 picture and enrollment form for validation.
* In case the library card was lost, a fee of ₱100 shall be charged for a replacement.
* The borrower’s card is non-transferable. Anybody caught presenting a card that is not his/her own shall be subjected to disciplinary action that was indicated in the general policy of the student manual.
## **COLLECTION DEVELOPMENT POLICY**
This Collection Development discusses the selection and collection practices of the Gordon College Library and Instructional Media Center. Specifically, it provides a written statement of the plan, providing details of the guidance of the library staff in terms of the acquisition, deselection, and item. By following the guidelines established in the program, the librarian can make more consistent and informed decisions about the collection and provide continuity during times of staff turnover.  
The availability of a collection development program for GC-LIMC is a serious intention for building its relevant collection. The policy will always serve as a guide in the selection of books and other materials responsive to the total community needs, its faculty, students, and its researchers.
**Purposes of the Collection Development Policy**
* Serve as a guide for the efficient and effective selection, acquisition, assessment and evaluation, deselection, and preservation of the library materials.
* Serve as a communication tool that clarifies objectives to library staff, and contributes to operational efficiency in terms of routine decisions
* Provide information for allocation of funds
* Facilitate coordination and cooperation among library and with other libraries
* Serve as a basis for prescribing appropriate collection development actions for the future.
## **OVERVIEW OF THE COLLECTION**
The College Library collection consists of resources in various formats defined by the curricular, teaching, and research needs of the College.
**A. Scope and Coverage of the Collection**
1. Course-related materials  
   a) The LIMC endeavors to acquire all appropriate course-related materials.  
   b) Faculty members are responsible for ensuring the timely supply of reading lists to the library.
2. Research Material  
   a) Research material is acquired to support departmental research interests.  
   b) The College Library will not acquire material for the exclusive use of individuals or offices.
3. Supplementary material  
   a) Supplementary material is acquired as background reading for student course-and project work and in developing research fields
**B. Types and Formats of Library Resources**
1. Reference collection: encyclopedias, dictionaries, bibliographies, atlases, concordances, indexes, abstracts, standard treatises, & important outlines of subject fields.
2. General book collection: materials that satisfy the general reading function of the library; books that cut across academic disciplines, books that are important regardless of their immediate relationship to the curriculum.
3. Serials: the library shall acquire serials according to the following guidelines:  
   a) Leading serials in the fields of interest of the College.  
   b) Serials devoted to the discussions of public affairs.  
   c) Serials containing substantial factual information concerning economic, political, spiritual & social events and scientific knowledge.  
   d) Serials of research value  
   e) Publications of the College  
   f) Newspapers
4. Government publications: foreign & local, including publications of organizations, especially those with international standing.
5. Pamphlets: these, together with clippings should be collected & organized to supplement the reference collection.
6. Dissertations & Theses: copies of all dissertations & theses written by students of the University should be collected in the library. Those from other universities may be gathered when the opportunity presents itself.
7. Non-book materials: these should likewise be gathered, including but not limited to:
   * Graphic materials: graphs, drawings, charts, etc.
   * Photographic materials: photographs, prints, pictures
   * Microfilm: micro prints, microcards, as necessary
   * Slides, motion pictures, newsreels
   * Auditory materials: records (disc or tapes)
   * Electronic resources (e-books, online journals, Newspaper in DVD format)
8. Textbooks: because of the repetition of information frequently included in them & because they are often quickly updated, in general, the library will only acquire textbooks as references.
9. Local history: the library shall build comprehensive collections on Olongapo and Gordon College culture and history.

# Proper Usage of Glass Boards
[Image: Proper Usage of Glass Boards]
**Thursday, May 23, 2024**
GUIDELINES FOR THE USE OF GLASS BOARD INSTALLED IN THE CLASSROOMS
* Use White Board Marker only to write on it
* Always erase marker ink from the surface after each use to prevent stains and ghosting.
* Don’t use dirty eraser or cloth, it will start spread ink residue across the surface of the board.
* Glass board must be cleared of any markings before the next class starts.
* Avoid using tapes or adhesives on the glass surface. These materials may be difficult and cause permanent damage to the glass board.
* Don’t use your hands to wipe a glass board! Your skin carries natural oils that over time leave a build-up of oil residue on the board. This can eventually ruin the surface of the glass board.
* Do not use sharp objects to avoid scratching or damaging the board surface.
* If the glass board is accidentally broken, it must be replaced with the same brand within seven (7) days from the date it was broken. If the item with specific brand and specification is not available, it must be replaced with three (3) timex of the quantity or in quantity comparable to the price of the item. The brand and specification must be approved first by the concerned personnel before replacement.


### Source: https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/%f0%9d%90%8e%f0%9d%90%92%f0%9d%90%96%f0%9d%90%92-%f0%9d%90%8f%f0%9d%90%84%f0%9d%90%92%f0%9d%90%8e-%f0%9d%90%83%f0%9d%90%8e%f0%9d%90%8b%f0%9d%90%84-%f0%9d%90%a4%f0%9d%90%a2%f0%9d%90%9c%f0%9d%90%a4

# 𝐎𝐒𝐖𝐒, 𝐏𝐄𝐒𝐎, 𝐃𝐎𝐋𝐄 𝐤𝐢𝐜𝐤 𝐨𝐟𝐟 𝐋𝐄𝐆𝐒 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟓
[Image: 𝐎𝐒𝐖𝐒, 𝐏𝐄𝐒𝐎, 𝐃𝐎𝐋𝐄 𝐤𝐢𝐜𝐤 𝐨𝐟𝐟 𝐋𝐄𝐆𝐒 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟓]
[OSWS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/osws/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Thursday, May 29, 2025**
The Office of Student Welfare and Services (OSWS) in partnership with the Department of Labor and Employment (DOLE) and Public Employment Service Office (PESO), conducted their annual Labor Education for Graduating Students (LEGS) 2025 yesterday, May 28, at the SMX Convention Center Olongapo.
The morning session included students from the College of Business and Accountancy (CBA) and the College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) assisted by the Samahan ng mga Mag-aaral sa Araling Panlipunan (SMAP).
Meanwhile, the event continued until afternoon for the graduating students of College of Education, Arts and Sciences (CEAS), College of Computer Studies (CCS), and College of Allied Health Studies (CAHS).
Started by Mr. Arvin Fabian, Senior Labor Employment Officer of DOLE, the seminar aims to equip its participants with sufficient knowledge regarding labor standards.
“Ang mandate po kasi ng DOLE is to ensure that everyone receive[s] what is due to them,” assured Mr. Fabian.
Additionally, this event was led by Ms. Jehan Doblado, Career Education and Job Placement Services Unit (CEJPSU) Coordinator, with the goal of preparing graduating studentsfor the corporate world.
“Para hindi sila zero knowledge. [Para] pagka-graduate nila alam nila ang gagawin nila,” the CEJPSU Coordinator said.
In her opening remarks, Ms. Lovelyn Ceralde, the Vice President of the Office of Student Welfare and Services (OSWS), stressed how necessary it is to study these standards.
“It is important that you, our future leaders and contributors, are well-versed in these fundamental principles,” said Ms. Ceralde.
She also highlighted the upcoming Commencement Exercises in July for the graduating batch 2025.
“See you on July 9 with your black togas!” the OSWS Vice President noted.
In an interview with The Forefront, some participants from different departments shared their insights on how the LEGS 2025 helped them as a graduating student of Gordon College.
“Ang laking tulong nitong seminar saken as a graduating student para maging prepared sa mga kakailanganin ko as a first time job seeker,” Jordan Magbanua, CCS Graduating Student claimed.
“Very helpful para sa mga graduating students lalo na sa akin kasi sa personal experience ko na naging employee na nag-resign para magpatuloy ang pag-aaral. Na-enlighten ako sa seminar na ito,” CEAS Graduating Student said.
Moreover, this event eyes recurrence every year in face-to-face set-up to ensure that graduating students remain well-informed regarding their rights as current or future employees.

# Hindi pula’t dilaw ang tunay na magkalaban
[Image: Hindi pula’t dilaw ang tunay na magkalaban]
[CAHS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/cahs/) [CBA](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/cba/) [CCS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ccs/) [CEAS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ceas/) [CHTM](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/chtm/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Sunday, September 7, 2025**
Gordon College, OLONGAPO CITY—Ibinandera nina Lourd Cedric Alejo ng College of Business and Accountancy (CBA) at Pauline Batinga ng College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) ang kani-kanilang kolehiyo matapos koronahan bilang bagong Lakan at Lakambini ng Buwan ng Wika 2025 noong ika-4 ng Sityembre.
Bukod sa prestihiyosong korona, itinanghal ding Lakan ng Larawan 2025 si Alejo habang nasungkit naman ni Rowena Ablian mula sa College of Allied Health Studies ang titulo ng Lakambini ng Larawan 2025.
Kinilala namang Lakan at Lakambini ng Pormal na Kasuotan sina Matthew Navarro (CHTM) at Shanelle Ferrer ng College of Computer Studies (CCS).
Batay sa kanilang ranggo, hinirang ang mga sumusunod:
Lakan at Lakambini ng Wika (1st Runner-up)
Matthew Navarro (CHTM) at Rowena Ablian (CAHS)
Lakan at Lakambini ng Panitikan (2nd Runner-up)
Henry Agustin Baja Jr. (CAHS) at Shanelle Ferrer (CCS)
Lakan at Lakambini ng Sining (3rd Runner-up)
Reyelle Ariza (CEAS) at Angel Mae Deseo (CEAS)
Lakan at Lakambini ng SamFil (4th Runner-up)
Clark Justin Pambid (CCS) at Luisa May Manlapat (CBA)
Bilang bahagi ng taunang pagdiriwang ng Buwan ng Wika, ang naturang patimpalak ay isinagawa sa pangunguna ng Samahang Filipino (SamFil). | via Keane Rafael Abdon/The FOREFRONT

# GC marks a milestone in ALCUCOA 2025
[Image: GC marks a milestone in ALCUCOA 2025]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, December 3, 2025**
The three-day program comes to an end as Gordon College (GC) conducts an exit conference for the Association of Local Colleges and Universities Commission on Accreditation (ALCUCOA) last November 28, at the Gordon College Main Building, Function Hall.
Vying for one Level III program, five Level II programs, and two candidate programs this year, the institution’s big step is a leap for development, embodying character, excellence, and service along the way.
“This is not the end,” Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano opened the exit conference of this year’s accreditation with a firm statement.
The accreditors commended the institution’s strengths including the Library Ambassadors Organization (LAO), community extension programs, and professionalism.
Meanwhile, they also recommended improvements such as stronger programs to supplement both students’ and teachers’ needs, more tangible and sustainable community extensions, and subjects that focus on application rather than theories.
As Dr. Elizabeth Montero, the supervising accreditor said in her message, “The new oil now is consistency,” a reminder to stay true and stand into the lenses of continuous development where growth is evident and seen.
In response, Ms. Geraldine Raydanas and Mrs. Rowenalyn Sagun expressed their utmost gratitude and promised to improve in the areas mentioned.
Ending the program, Dr. Kristin Joy Mendoza, secretary of GC’s Board of Trustees, left a valuable statement regarding institutionalization and sustainability.
“Every accreditation is an opportunity to innovate and fill the spaces where we can do better,” Dr. Mendoza emphasized.
Meanwhile, the results of the 2025 accreditation are set to be announced in the next few weeks. | via Mikhaela Sagun/The FOREFRONT

# CHED validates the MAN program of Institute of Graduate Studies
[Image: CHED validates the MAN program of Institute of Graduate Studies]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Thursday, June 19, 2025**
The CHED Technical Panel for Nursing, Technical Evaluator for Nursing, and Regional Quality Assessment Team (RQAT) conducted onsite validation at Gordon College, Institute of Graduate Studies, last May 29-30, 2025.
The Gordon College welcomed evaluators from the Commission on Higher Education (CHED)—Regional Quality Assessment Team (RQAT) for an on-site validation aimed at securing a Certificate of Program Compliance (COPC) for the Master of Arts in Nursing (MAN) program.
Program Chair for MAN, Dr. Victor C. Quimen Jr., introduced the CHED-RQAT evaluators: Dr. Maria Martina Geraldine Dimalibot, Dr. Al D. Biag, Dr. Mary Ann Gienette D. Medina, and Ms. Niña Jessa Angeli Palaganas.
The evaluators conducted thorough assessments of program administration, faculty credentials, curriculum, student services, facilities, and resources. Their findings and recommendations, discussed in a post-evaluation conference, will guide Gordon College, Institute of Graduate Studies continuing efforts to enhance academic quality.
Preparations were led by Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano, Dean of the Institute of Graduate Studies; Dr. Darwin P. Paguio, Vice President for Academic Affairs; Dr. Narcisa R. Figuerres, Assistant Dean of the Institute of Graduate Studies; and Dr. Victor C. Quimen Jr. Program Chair for MAN, Ms. Joan Joy A. De Vera; Executive Assistant of the Institute of Graduate Studies, Ms. Diana Grace M. Vallar; Executive Assistant of the Academic Affairs Office; and strong support from other college officials and faculty.
Gordon College, Institute of Graduate Studies, conveyed its sincere gratitude to the CHED-RQAT team for their invaluable insights and steadfast support, recognizing the visit as a major step in the institution’s ongoing journey toward academic excellence.

# “2,000 change-makers” attend NSTP grad ‘25
[Image: “2,000 change-makers” attend NSTP grad ‘25]
[NSTP](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/nstp/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Sunday, June 8, 2025**
Two thousand Gordon College (GC) students enrolled in their National Service and Training Program (NSTP) courses filled the SMX Convention Center, Olongapo City, on Wednesday, for this year’s NSTP Graduation Ceremony.
With the theme, “Volunteerism in Action: Shaping Leaders, Strengthening Community”, this year’s batch of NSTP graduates was graced by Miss Manila 2014 Dr. Krischelle D. Halili.
Dr. Halili emphasized that leadership is an “act not for yourself, but for others” in her address. “Serve something greater than yourself and take your friends with you.”
Moreover, she called on students to love the country and God while also channeling ‘tech natives’ to participate in online community initiatives as well as community services and university council activities on-site.
Leading the initial confirmation of the graduates, Vice President for Academic Affairs Dr. Darwin P. Paguio also delivered his special message, focusing on the impact of genuine leadership.
“True leadership is not defined merely by title or accolades but by the impact you make in the lives of others,” he said as he congratulated the “two thousand change-makers” afterward.
This was followed by the awarding of certificates to Literary Training Service (LTS) and Civic Welfare Training Service (CWTS) completers from the different departments of the institution.
NSTP Instructor Mr. Hubert Lew Magrata delivered his closing remarks to officially close the program.

# Mentors did not give up on the violent carry of Sharks
[Image: Mentors did not give up on the violent carry of Sharks]

The College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS) Mentors brought home the championship for Men’s Futsal against the College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) Sharks with a score of 1-0.

# Gender and Development and Mental Health Awareness Seminar
[Image: Gender and Development and Mental Health Awareness Seminar]
[Extension](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/extension/) [GAD](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/gad/) [RDCES](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/rdces/) [Community Extension](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/community-extension/)
**Thursday, May 25, 2023**
The GC CESU, in partnership with the GC GAD Unit and the GC Guidance Office, successfully organized a comprehensive seminar. The event consisted of morning and afternoon sessions and actively involved grade 12 senior high school students from Tapinac Senior High School. Held at the GC Function Hall, the seminar provided an ideal platform for the exchange of valuable insights and knowledge on these important subjects. The presence of esteemed resource speakers, Ms. Abigail Legaspi (GAD Unit Head), Ms. Mechel Bautista (GC Guidance Office Head), and Ms. JaimJaime Nhilen Apostol (GC Guidance Designate), contributed to the seminar’s excellent delivery.

# CHTM Sharks, sinaliwan ang ritmo tungong panalo
[Image: CHTM Sharks, sinaliwan ang ritmo tungong panalo]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Monday, September 15, 2025**
GORDON COLLEGE, OLONGAPO CITY—Nagliyab sa entablado ang College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) Sharks sa Dancesports Competition—Latin Category nang ipamalas ang kanilang husay sa Cha-cha na sumubok sa indayog ng mga ritmo, Rumba na nagbigay-diin sa senswalidad, at Jive na nagpaigting ng sigla at enerhiya ng SIKLAB 2025.
Matapos ang tatlong antas ng laban, nangibabaw sina John Michael Apan at Cathlyn Heart Del Rosario ng CHTM Sharks. Ipinamalas nila ang matinding dedikasyon, tiyaga, at koordinasyon sa bawat hakbang at bawat himig ng musika. Tinanghal silang kampeon ng gabi sa kategoryang Latin.
Sinundan naman ito ng College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS) Mentors na sumungkit sa ikalawang pwesto at College of Business and Accountancy (CBA) Wizards sa ikatlong pwesto.
“Nagpapasalamat po ako sa Diyos sa guidance, also sa lakas. Unexpected po yung nangyari, aminado naman po kami na lahat ng department malalakas, also dahil nga po last year hindi namin nakuha ‘yong gold. Mas pinag-igihan na lang po namin yung training namin. Sobrang saya po namin na champion po kami,” ani ni John Michael, sa naramdamang galak matapos magwagi.
Dagdag pa ni Cathlyn, natural lang sa mag-partner ang mag-away. Hindi man daw nagkakasundo sa gustong steps; mga routines, mga gustong minsa’y hindi nauunawan. Ngunit, nagagawan naman ng paraan at naaayos sa usapan.
Hindi lamang sa tropeyo at ranggo umikot ang laban. Sa huli, ipinakita ng Latin Category ang sigasig at diwa ng komunidad ng Gordon College—kung saan ang bawat indak ay larawan ng determinasyon, pagkakaisa, at pagmamahal sa sining ng sayaw ng Gordon College Community. | via Lianne Malazarte/The FOREFRONT
\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_
Kuha ni Franz Feria
Layout ni John Patrick Mateo, Lance Isaac Leon
ERRATUM: The image used in the publication material before was another representative of CHTM. The champions of the Latin category are now shown in the edited material. We sincerely apologize for the confusion it may have caused.

# Fostering Mental Health
[Image: Fostering Mental Health]
[Extension](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/extension/) [RDCES](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/rdces/) [Community Extension](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/community-extension/)
**Wednesday, July 26, 2023**
In adherence to our commitment to community service, the GC Community Extension Services Unit (GC-CESU), in collaboration with the GC Guidance Office and Tapinac Senior High School, successfully organized and conducted a seminar on July 31, 2023. The seminar aimed to enhance the resilience of the attendees by equipping them with techniques to address and manage challenges, emphasizing the importance of self-care, addressing mental health stigma, and facilitating access to mental health services. Ms. Mechel Bautista, GC Guidance Office Head, served as the resource speaker. The seminar saw active participation from 42 teachers from Tapinac Senior High School and took place at the Gordon College function hall.

# Artistry and Unity Take Center Stage at PASE Community Concert 2026
[Image: Artistry and Unity Take Center Stage at PASE Community Concert 2026]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, February 25, 2026**
The PASE Community Concert 2026, held on the 2nd day of the 27th Founding Anniversary celebration and in observance of National Arts Month, became a memorable showcase of talent and creativity. The program featured performances from Pase Ballo, Pase Voci, Pase Teatro, and Pase Cuerdas.
The concert opened with dance performances from PASE Ballo, followed by a singing presentation from PASE Voci, and an acting performance from PASE Teatro, with each group having its own spotlight to showcase their artistry and skills. It marked the first time that all PASE groups performed together on one stage while highlighting their unique talents individually.
The presentation celebrated unity, creativity, and the passion for the arts, making the community concert a meaningful part of the anniversary and National Arts Month celebration.

# THE 4TH GORDON COLLEGE DISTINGUISHED ALUMNI SERVICE AWARD: Call for Nomination 2025
[Image: THE 4TH GORDON COLLEGE DISTINGUISHED ALUMNI SERVICE AWARD: Call for Nomination 2025]
[Alumni](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/alumni/)
**Monday, August 4, 2025**
**GENERAL INFORMATION AND GUIDELINES**
Pursuant to Gordon College Alumni Association, Inc. (GCAAI) Board Resolution No. 4, series of 2022, the GCAAI establishes the DISTINGUISHED ALUMNI SERVICE AWARD (DASA) to honor Gordon College graduates who have excelled both in their professional and personal careers and who have served as an example to other alumni, students, and the community, both locally and globally. Starting this year, the GCAAI Board of Trustees will recognize the exemplary service and distinguished achievements that brought honor to the Institution and have made significant contributions to society. The GC-DASA recipients will exemplify the core values that the Institution uphold since its establishment: Exceptional Character, Remarkable Excellence, And Unselfish Service.  
The GC-DASA shall be the highest honor bestowed by the Alumni Association and will be an event to be celebrated annually as part of the GC Founding Anniversary and/or GC Alumni Homecoming.  
**GENERAL GUIDELINES:**
1. Only graduates of Gordon College (or GC, formerly Olongapo City Colleges) are eligible for the GC-DASA. The nominee shall be a member of the Gordon College Alumni Association, Inc. as confirmed by its Board.
2. Any GC graduate, faculty member, or stakeholders may make nominations for the GC-DASA. Self–  
   nominations are accepted. The nominee shall accomplish the Official Nomination Form to be provided by the GCAAI. No limitation will be placed on the total number of nominations accepted for consideration.
3. The nominee shall have no pending administrative or criminal case upon nomination. He/she shall exhibit a high degree of character & integrity.
4. Only one recipient per college/program will be awarded. In case the college/program will have no  
   nominees/recipients, the awarding ceremony will still commence. Likewise, the GCAAI Board of Trustees can directly, without prejudice to the respective college, nominate and evaluate for such an award though the college/program have already a candidate.
5. Only living candidates are considered. In the event of the death of a candidate between notification of the award and the award ceremony, the award may be given posthumously with the permission of the family and the GCAAI. In the event that the awardee declines to accept the award and/or fails to attend the awarding ceremonies, the GCAAI reserves the right to withdraw the award.
6. A GC graduate sitting as a member of the selection committee or a member of the GCAAI Board of Trustees is not eligible to receive the award. This individual is eligible for nomination after a one-year waiting period from the date he/she steps down from his/her role.
7. Recipients are only eligible for one GC-DASA in a lifetime.
8. The recipients will receive a plaque or certificate of recognition and will be posted on the official FB page of the GCAAI. It will announce the annual recipients through various methods and honor each recipient with the appropriate recognition.
9. If for any reason, the GCAAI is not able to conduct the Awards as planned (including due to tampering, unauthorized intervention, fraud, technical difficulties, pandemic, or any other causes beyond the control of the organizer which impact the administration, security, fairness, integrity or proper conduct of the Awards), then they reserve the right in their sole discretion to make any reasonable variation to the Awarding Ceremony.
10. The Nomination Committee and/or GCAAI Board of Trustees may disqualify any nominee that they  
    reasonably determine has breached any of the terms and conditions or otherwise engaged in unlawful or other improper conduct that may bring the Awards into disrepute or jeopardize the fair and proper conduct of the Awards.
11. Other guidelines may be promulgated as decided by the GCAAI Board of Trustees.
**NOMINATION COMMITTEE:**  
The Nomination Committee (NC) may be organized by the Dean/OIC in every college and/or the GCAAI. The committee must be composed of at least three (3) members from their respective colleges. The total count of committee members should be an odd number. The chairperson of the NC shall be the Dean or its duly authorized representative and for the GCAAI, any member can initiate such nomination. No member of the NC must be related within the second degree of consanguinity or affinity to any of the nominees/candidates for awards. If the Dean/OIC is related within the second degree of consanguinity or affinity to any of the candidates for awards, the Dean/OIC must inhibit him/herself from participating in the process. The approval shall come from the person next in rank.  
The NC shall:
1. Abide by the criteria set forth by the GCAAI.
2. For nominees endorsed by the College, it shall issue a certificate of authenticity to all the documents that will be submitted to the GCAAI.
3. Deliberate on the qualifications of the nominees for the awards based on the criteria and the documents submitted.
4. Recommend to the GCAAI Board of Trustees or its Alumni Association President the result of nomination for approval.
5. Recommend to the GCAAI Board of Trustees or its Alumni Association President the resolution on any related issue that may arise from the results of the awards.
6. Ensure that the guidelines stipulated herein are followed.
**NOMINATION AND SELECTION PROCESS:**
1. Complete the online/hard copy Nomination Form.
2. Nominators will receive an auto-generated copy via email of what they submitted, i.e., the nomination form including any attachments. In any case, it is not available, the NC may determine what means of notification it will conduct to the nominators to confirm the submission of the nomination form and its attachments.
3. Nominees and nominators will be contacted by the NC/GCAAI within three days of receipt of the  
   nomination to acknowledge receipt.
4. A nominee may be asked to clarify or submit further information and evidence to assist the NC/GCAAI in assessing the nomination at any stage of the selection process.
5. The NC/GCAAI may also seek verification of the nominee’s achievements from their current or previous employer or other relevant individual or organization representative. By being nominated for this Award, the nominees are giving consent to the NC/GCAAI to contact these individuals to verify nominations.
6. Nominations will be reviewed and shortlisted by the NC/GCAAI and nominees who have not been  
   shortlisted will be notified. The GC-DASA candidates will be asked to submit a brief profile for this purpose. The candidate shall garner a total score of 60 points to be included in the shortlist. If a nominee cannot meet the required points, he/she is automatically excluded from the shortlist.
7. Every member of the NC will recommend/endorse the final candidate to the GCAAI President and then, the GCAAI Board of Trustees, sitting as Awards Selection Committee, will issue a confirmation through a Board Resolution. The report on the results of the NC shall be signed by all members of the committee and certified by the chairperson.
8. In any case that such protest will be filed during the confirmation of the final candidates, the final decision on the protest shall be decided by the GCAAI Board of Trustees. The GCAAI Board of Trustees’ decision is final on all matters relating to the nomination process and the selection of award recipients, and they are not required to provide feedback as to why a nomination was unsuccessful.
Call for Nominations: 4th Distinguished Alumni Service Award (DASA)
The Gordon College Alumni Association, Inc. proudly announces the opening of nominations for the 4th Distinguished Alumni Service Award (DASA), which will run from August 4 to 22, 2025. This prestigious recognition honors outstanding alumni who have demonstrated exceptional service, leadership, and commitment to their profession, community, or alma mater. We encourage all alumni, faculty, and stakeholders to submit nominations for individuals who embody the values and excellence upheld by Gordon College. For complete nomination guidelines, kindly scan the QR code below.
The awarding ceremony will be held on October 11, 2025. Let us come together to recognize and celebrate the achievements of Gordon College alumni who continue to make a lasting difference.

# GC Welcomes Konyang University of South Korea
[Image: GC Welcomes Konyang University of South Korea]
[IPDEA](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ipdea/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, May 14, 2025**
On May 14, 2025, Gordon College warmly welcomed delegates from Konyang University, South Korea, as part of an initiative to explore academic collaboration between the two institutions. The delegation was composed of Ms. Youngmi Sohn, Head of the Global Education Innovation Headquarters, and Mr. Dong-Yeon Ko, Team Leader.
The visit aimed to strengthen international ties, particularly in the field of Allied Health Studies, and included a tour of the college’s facilities—classrooms, laboratories, and library—especially in relation to Konyang’s interest in developing a short-term language program.
During the meeting, the delegates expressed their interest in discussing various opportunities for collaboration and confirmed that they are currently reviewing the proposed Memorandum of Agreement (MOA). Both institutions expressed optimism for a sustainable and meaningful partnership that supports global academic engagement and student development.

# Cosme Y. Sering Library is Now Open
[Image: Cosme Y. Sering Library is Now Open]
[LIMC](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/limc/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Saturday, January 18, 2025**
Today marks a significant milestone for Gordon College, as we celebrate the blessing of the newly renovated Cosme Y. Sering Library. Made possible through the generous donation of the Acevedo Family, this library serves as a vital resource for students, faculty, and the Olongapo City community, promoting learning, collaboration, and access to knowledge.
The Acevedo Family’s generosity exemplifies their commitment to education and community development. This improved facility enhances academic opportunities and enriches the educational experience for students and stakeholders alike.
We extend our deepest gratitude to the Acevedo Family, Hon. Rolen C. Paulino, Former City Mayor, Hon. Rolen C. Paulino Jr., Olongapo City Mayor, Hon/ Cristabelle Marie “Tata” Paulino, Committee on Education Chairperson, Hon. Sarah Lugerna Lipumano-Garcia, City Councilor, Hon. Benjamin G. Cajudo II, Former City Councilor, Dr. Lora Yusi, CHEDROIII Director, Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano, OIC-College President, the City Officials, Board of Trustees, College Officials, College Personnel, Student Leaders, and everyone who contributed to this meaningful endeavor.
With its legacy of excellence and commitment to progress, Gordon College remains steadfast in providing quality education and opportunities for its students and the community. Together, we celebrate the transformative power of education and its enduring impact on society.

# GC JOB FAIR 2024
[Image: GC JOB FAIR 2024]
[OSWS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/osws/)
**Wednesday, February 7, 2024**
In celebration of the Gordon College 25th Founding Anniversary, the GC Community Extension Services Unit and the Bachelor of Science in Business Administration—Major in Human Resource Management—in collaboration with PESO and DOLE of Olongapo City, along with the participation of GC Alumni and Linkages, Companies, Ayala Malls Harbor Point—Subic, and Subic Bay Metropolitan Authority, are set to organize the GC Job Fair 2024. # SUCs still face budget cuts in proposed 2026 national budget – Kabataan
[Image: SUCs still face budget cuts in proposed 2026 national budget – Kabataan]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Saturday, October 18, 2025**
“Sa ibang institusyon o unibersidad [man], ang matapyasan ng budget para sa sektor ng edukasyon ay isang paraan upang mawalan ng karapatan ang mga magaaral na magkaroon ng kalidad na edukasyon,” a 4th Year BACOMM Student exclaimed.
Twenty-five State Universities and Colleges (SUCs) are still facing budget cuts amounting to Php 6.88 billion after Congress rejected amendments by Kabataan Partylist in its second-to-last hearing of the proposed 2026 national budget.
In a Facebook Post, youth-led Kabataan partylist condemns the House of Representative’s rejection of proposed amendments to House Bill (HB) No. 4058 or the proposed 2026 national budget that would have alleviated deficits to SUCs.
“Mariing tinututulan ng Kabataan Partylist ang pahayag ng Kongreso na sapat na raw ang dagdag-badyet sa SUCs at ang pagpondo sa 2022-2025 Free Higher Education (FHE) deficiencies. Danas ng bawat Iskolar ng Bayan ang hirap na magpatuloy sa kanilang pag-aaral dahil sa kakulangan ng pondo at suporta mula sa gobyerno,” the youth-led party-list said in a Facebook post.
Furthermore, in a report by Krixia Subingsubing for Philippine Daily Inquirer, twenty-five SUCs will be affected by budget cuts amounting to Php 6.88 billion. Three of these institutions are from Central Luzon including Aurora State College of Technology, Bulacan Agricultural State College, Central Luzon State University in Nueva Ecija and Philippine Merchant Marine Academy in Zambales.
Meanwhile, a group of school presidents and student regents from these institutions, led by Kabataan Party-list, has since issued a unity statement calling for Congress to restore budget cuts from the proposed national budget next year, citing “chronic underfunding” of the country’s education system.
According to the group, budget cuts compel SUCs to restrict admissions and compensate with “internally generated income from business ventures [and on] direct fee collection from students”.
As of October 10, the House has approved the proposed Php 6.7 trillion 2026 budget bill for 2026 following amendments. The budget is scheduled for a final reading in Congress on October 13 before congressional break. | via Lance Isaac Leon/The FOREFRONT
Sources: Kabataan Partylist/via FACEBOOK, Krixia Subingsubing/PHILIPPINE DAILY INQUIRER

# Gordon College Hosts Mabalacat City College for Benchmarking and Knowledge Sharing
[Image: Gordon College Hosts Mabalacat City College for Benchmarking and Knowledge Sharing]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, May 5, 2026**
Mabalacat City College (MCC) delegates visited Gordon College on May 5, 2026, at the Audio Visual Room (AVR) for a benchmarking and knowledge-sharing activity.
Gordon College President Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano, together with GC officials and staff, warmly welcomed the MCC delegation led by Dr. Myrna C. Calma, Vice President for Administration. The group met to learn and exchange ideas on key practices in administration, academics, quality assurance, research, student services, planning, and information systems.
This collaborative activity opens opportunities for shared learning and deeper understanding of institutional practices that contribute to service development and innovation.

# LCUAA 2025 Welcomes Rainy Sunday Afternoon
[Image: LCUAA 2025 Welcomes Rainy Sunday Afternoon]
[LCUAA](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/lcuaa/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Sunday, October 19, 2025**
The eagerly anticipated week-long 14th LCUAA National Games officially kicked off today at the City of San Jose del Monte (CSJDM) Sports Complex.
Cheers and applause filled the venue as the opening ceremony began with a parade featuring all 27 participating colleges, each led by their institution’s gold medalists.
Following the parade was the introduction of the LCUAA 2025 representative officers, a doxology by the Sining Tanglawan ng San Jose del Monte Performing Group, the singing of the National Anthem and the San Jose del Monte city hymn, and an energetic dance performance by the San Joseño World Class Talents troupe.
City College of San Jose del Monte (CCSJDM) College President Dr. Roger S. Ambida delivered the opening remarks, warmly welcoming delegates to the institution.
Hon. Arturo B. Robes then gave the keynote address, encouraging athletes to build a brighter and more empowered future.
Additionally, Dr. Lora L. Yusi, CHED Region 3 Director IV, gave an inspiring speech, highlighting the significance of LCUAA 2025 as a testament to the growth of local universities: “We see scholars who dream larger than themselves.”
The competitive events officially begin tomorrow, October 20, 2025.
Stay tuned to the official Facebook page of The Forefront for updates on the 14th LCUAA National Games. | via Alyssa Marie Alcantara / The FOREFRONT

# The celebration of arts and culture ignites a fiery PASE Community Concert this 27th Founding Anniversary
[Image: The celebration of arts and culture ignites a fiery PASE Community Concert this 27th Founding Anniversary]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, February 25, 2026**
The Performing Arts Student Ensemble (PASE) ignites a synergy of performances on the second day of the 27th Founding Anniversary on February 25, at East Tapinac Oval Grounds.
The first segment featured PASE Ballo’s contemporary dance performance to the song ‘Tala,’ touching the hearts of GCians with their moving steps.
Furthermore, PASE Voci’s chorale performance filled the air with harmonic voices and unity, creating an ethereal atmosphere through their cover of ‘Bagong Umaga’ by Bayang Barrios.
The third segment was a play by PASE Teatro, showcasing relatable acting that highlighted the reality of social media’s impact on people’s lives.
In addition, PASE Ballo returned for another performance of a cultural inspired dance filled with enthusiasm and energy.
Moreover, the fifth segment was a collaboration between all PASE ensembles — Cuerdas, Teatro, and Voci. Storytelling and music united on a narrative of Overseas Filipino Workers (OFWs) acted by Teatro and set to Voci and Cuerdas’s rendition of ‘Pinoy Ako’ by Bamboo.
Following that is a performance from Teatro’s newest troupe, the collectives. The four-member rap group sang Filipino hip-hop songs that captured the culture of Pinoy music history. They performed covers of ‘Venus’ by Ron Henely, ‘Ikaw lang’ by Kiyo, and ‘Upuan’ by Gloc 9, bringing back nostalgic vibes to many GCians.
Finally, the last segment featured a collaboration of Voci and Cuerdas. Following this is PASE Ballo’s dance performance, celebrating Olongapo’s culture and the jeepney tradition. The performance transitioned to Cuerdas and Voci, who performed a reggae song.
The event proudly celebrated National Arts Month this February, showcasing the diverse talents and artistry of GCians. Through various performances, the students highlighted their creativity and passion for the arts, demonstrating the vibrant cultural landscape of the institution. It was a testament to the rich artistic expression and talent that GCians have, making it a memorable celebration of arts and culture, leaving a lasting impression on the audiences at the 27th founding anniversary. | via Lei Ydah Lasala Ledesma / The FOREFRONT
Photos by Franz Feria & Lauren Louise Sanita

# PRC On-site Caravan 2025 Lands with a Bang
[Image: PRC On-site Caravan 2025 Lands with a Bang]
[Alumni](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/alumni/) [CCS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ccs/) [IPDEA](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ipdea/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Friday, May 9, 2025**
𝐏𝐑𝐎𝐅𝐄𝐒𝐒𝐈𝐎𝐍𝐀𝐋 𝐑𝐄𝐆𝐔𝐋𝐀𝐓𝐈𝐎𝐍 𝐂𝐎𝐌𝐌𝐈𝐒𝐒𝐈𝐎𝐍 (𝐏𝐑𝐂) 𝐎𝐍-𝐒𝐈𝐓𝐄 𝐒𝐄𝐑𝐕𝐈𝐂𝐄 𝐂𝐀𝐑𝐀𝐕𝐀𝐍
GORDON COLLEGE | OLONGAPO CITY, ZAMBALES
May 9, 2025
[Image: ✨]
It’s a wrap!
[Image: ✨]
The PRC On-Site Service Caravan at Gordon College was a resounding success, delivering a total of 549 services to Professionals in a single day. This initiative significantly improved access to essential PRC services within the local community—and is part of our ongoing commitment to support not only our Alumni but also to all Professionals.
Breakdown of Services Rendered:
• Renewal – 138
• Initial Registration – 60
• Duplicate – 11
• Certification – 170
• Authentication – 162
• Certificate of Special Order (COS) – 8
TOTAL – 549
A special acknowledgment to the dedicated PRC Committee for their exemplary service and leadership:
Ms. Elaine Perez Espela, Ms. Glenda B. Sapon (Team Leader), Ms. Joana Benigna Pabelonia, Mr. Joselito Dulay, and Mr. Angelo Pedro Hernandez.
We are also grateful to the student organizations for their invaluable support and active participation:
• College of Computer Studies Student Council (CCS SC)
• GCCCS Empowered League of Information Technology Education Students (ELITES)
Sincere appreciation goes to the VP for Institutional Planning, Development, and External Affairs, Dr. Kristin Joy A. Mendoza, for her instrumental role in the successful implementation of this initiative.
We would also like to express our heartfelt thanks to Mr. Joseph Angelo B. Pusing GCAAI President and Ms. Denise Lou Punzalan for spearheading this event, and to the Board of Trustees and Working Committee of the Gordon College Alumni Association Inc. (GCAAI) for their full support and commitment in making this initiative a success.
Special thanks to Dr. Imelda DP Soriano, College President, for her unwavering support in bringing services closer to the community.
This caravan is not just a service initiative—it’s our way of helping empower our alumni community as they continue their professional journeys.
Thank you to everyone who contributed to the success of the PRC On-Site Service Caravan. Together, we continue to broaden access to professional services and empower our community.

# GC produces 9 new CPAs; yields 39.13% pass rate in October CPALE
[Image: GC produces 9 new CPAs; yields 39.13% pass rate in October CPALE]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, November 4, 2025**
Nine alumni from the College of Business and Accountancy (CBA) of Gordon College (GC) were among the passers of the October Certified Public Accountant Licensure Exam (CPALE) per Professional Regulatory Board of Accountancy, on November 4.
GC garnered an overall passing rate of 39.13% from 9 passers out of 23 takers, surpassing the national passing rate of 34.02%. These constitute the 3,460 newly registered Certified Public Accountants (CPA).
Comprising this are three first-time takers and six repeater examinees.
In addition, Mike Ronnel Castro of Pampanga State University (for. Don Honorario Ventura State University) topped the examination with a 91.83% rating.
Bicol University – Daraga is the lone institution in the list of top-performing schools with 50 or more examinees with an 84.85% passing rate.
The licensure exam was conducted from October 24 to 26. | via Keane Rafael Abdon/The FOREFRONT

# CESU Brigada Eskwela
[Image: CESU Brigada Eskwela]
[Extension](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/extension/) [RDCES](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/rdces/) [Community Extension](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/community-extension/)
**Thursday, August 17, 2023**
The close connection between the Bayanihan spirit and Brigada Eskwela highlights their shared focus on community service and volunteerism. The Bayanihan spirit embodies the collective collaboration of a community to attain common objectives, while Brigada Eskwela serves as a community service program uniting volunteers to improve school environments.
Upon receiving Kalalake Elementary School’s request to join the National Schools Maintenance Week, the institution and the dedicated BSEd English major student volunteers effectively carried out the clean-up drive at Kalalake Elementary School.
